                    - link "Open in a new tab" [ref=e1415] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: table/nutrients-13-03211-t002/
                    - paragraph [ref=e1418]: "Abbreviation: BMI, body mass index; HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c; IL-6/8/10/17α, interleukin-6/8/10/17α; IFN-γ, interferon-γ; PAI1, plasminogen activator inhibitor 1; SCFAs, short-chain fatty acids; TC, total cholesterol; TNF-α, tumor necrosis factor α."
                - generic [ref=e1419]:
                  - heading "4.2. Type 2 and Gestational Diabetes Mellitus" [level=3] [ref=e1420]
                  - paragraph [ref=e1421]:
                    - text: A clinical trial with 50 patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus showed that the daily intake of 120 g fermented milk containing
                    - emphasis [ref=e1422]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: BB-12 and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1423]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: La-5 (10
                    - superscript [ref=e1424]: "9"
                    - text: CFU for each) for 6 weeks improved serum levels of fructosamine, hemoglobin A1c (HbA1c), and IL-10 [
                    - link "120" [ref=e1425] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B120-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Moreover, both"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1426]: Lactobacillus reuteri
                    - text: ADR-1 and ADR-3 strain could decrease the levels of serum HbA1c and cholesterol with regulating
                    - emphasis [ref=e1427]: Bacteroidetes
                    - text: and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1428]: Bifidobacterium
                    - text: abundances in 68 patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus, and the ADR-3 strain revealed a better effect on decreasing blood pressure and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1429]: Firmicutes
                    - text: abundance than the ADR-1 strain [
                    - link "121" [ref=e1430] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B121-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Moreover, the intake of fermented milk processed by"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1431]: Lactobacillus casei
                    - text: Shirota for 16 weeks restricted the translocation of gut bacteria into blood circulation in 70 patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus, leading to the increased abundances of
                    - emphasis [ref=e1432]: Clostridium coccoides
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1433]: Clostridium leptum
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1434]: Lactobacillus
                    - text: in fecal [
                    - link "122" [ref=e1435] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B122-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Furthermore, several meta-analyses showed the benefits of probiotics treatment on improving HbA1c and fasting insulin ["
                    - link "142" [ref=e1436] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B142-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: ","
                    - link "143" [ref=e1437] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B143-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1438]:
                    - text: The effects of probiotics on gestational diabetes mellitus remained controversial [
                    - link "144" [ref=e1439] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B144-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. The clinical trials on pregnant women with normal body weight reported that the daily supplementation of"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1440]: Lactobacillus rhamnosus
                    - text: HN001 (6 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1441]: "9"
                    - text: CFU) could lower the risk and relapse prevalence of diabetes mellitus, whereas the probiotics containing
                    - emphasis [ref=e1442]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1443]: Lactobacillus rhamnosus
                    - text: did not prevent gestational diabetes mellitus in pregnant women with overweight or obesity [
                    - link "123" [ref=e1444] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B123-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: ","
                    - link "124" [ref=e1445] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B124-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Further evidence from 230 pregnant women with obesity revealed that the daily intake of probiotics containing"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1446]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: BB12 and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1447]: Lactobacillus rhamnosus
                    - text: GG (6.5 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1448]: "9"
                    - text: CFU for each) for 36 weeks did not improve depression, anxiety, and physical well-being status during pregnancy [
                    - link "126" [ref=e1449] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B126-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. So, the bodyweight condition might be the critical factor for the effects of probiotics on gestational diabetes mellitus ["
                    - link "125" [ref=e1450] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B125-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1451]:
                    - text: Prebiotics also exerted a potential in the prevention of type 2 diabetes mellitus by modulating gut microbiota dysbiosis [
                    - link "145" [ref=e1452] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B145-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: ","
                    - link "146" [ref=e1453] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B146-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. A randomized placebo-controlled trial on 46 patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus showed that the intake of 10 g/day oligofructose-enriched inulin for 2 months improved glycemic status, lipid profiles, and immune biomarkers ["
                    - link "129" [ref=e1454] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B129-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Besides, the effects of synbiotic supplementation on patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus attracted more and more attention, and the"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1455]: Coix lacryma-jobi
                    - text: could enhance the effect of probiotic yogurt in reducing body weight and fasting blood glucose [
                    - link "133" [ref=e1456] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B133-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Besides, another clinical trial indicated that synbiotics intervention just revealed a limited benefit in diabetic-related biomarkers without improving glucose metabolism ["
                    - link "134" [ref=e1457] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B134-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Moreover, a 9-week intake of 500 mg/day synbiotics containing"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1458]: Bifidobacterium
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1459]: Lactobacillus
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1460]: Streptococcus thermophilus
                    - text: ", and fructo-oligosaccharide only improved the HbA1c, BMI, and microalbuminuria in 70 patients with type 2 diabetes mellitus, without changing fasting blood glucose, lipid profiles, and creatinine ["
                    - link "135" [ref=e1461] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B135-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Furthermore, a clinical trial compared the effects between probiotics ("
                    - emphasis [ref=e1462]: Bifidobacterium bifidum
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1463]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1464]: Bifidobacterium longum
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1465]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: ", 1.5 × 10"
                    - superscript [ref=e1466]: "9"
                    - text: CFU for each) and synbiotics (the probiotics plus inulin) on 120 prediabetic adults, turned out that a 6-month intake of synbiotics failed to regulate gut microbial composition [
                    - link "136" [ref=e1467] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B136-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1468]: In general, probiotics alleviated type 2 diabetes mellitus, but the clinical application of probiotics in gestational diabetes mellitus with obesity needs further investigation. Besides, the application of bacterial-processed prebiotics is an emerging trend in clinical exploration, and the optimal formula of synbiotics as well as the specific efficacy of postbiotics on diabetes mellitus still needs more tests.
                - generic [ref=e1469]:
                  - heading "4.3. Other Metabolic Diseases" [level=3] [ref=e1470]
                  - paragraph [ref=e1471]:
                    - text: Metabolic impairment is a common complication of nervous system dysfunction like Alzheimer’s disease and depression [
                    - link "147" [ref=e1472] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B147-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: ","
                    - link "148" [ref=e1473] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B148-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. A clinical trial on 60 patients with Alzheimer’s disease showed that a 12-week intake of probiotic milk containing"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1474]: Bifidobacterium bifidum
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1475]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1476]: Lactobacillus casei
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1477]: Lactobacillus fermentum
                    - text: at a dose of 200 mL/day (2 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1478]: "9"
                    - text: CFU/g for each) improved cognitive function and metabolic status [
                    - link "127" [ref=e1479] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B127-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Likely, an 8-week consumption of probiotics ("
                    - emphasis [ref=e1480]: Bifidobacterium bifidum
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1481]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1482]: Lactobacillus casei
                    - text: ", 2 × 10"
                    - superscript [ref=e1483]: "9"
                    - text: CFU/g for each) improved metabolic status with 40 patients with major depressive disorders [
                    - link "128" [ref=e1484] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B128-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1485]:
                    - text: Prebiotics could also benefit obesity-related major depressive disorder, and the patient who received an additional 10 g/day of inulin showed a better improvement in fat mass and TC level compared to the patient who received a calorie-restricted diet alone [
                    - link "130" [ref=e1486] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B130-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Besides, a double-blind, placebo-control clinical trial with 75 NAFLD patients showed that the consumption of prebiotic inulin at doses of 10 g/day for 3 months could improve the grade of fatty liver and the serum levels of aminotransferase enzymes ["
                    - link "131" [ref=e1487] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B131-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1488]:
                    - text: Synbiotics supplement is a promising way for controlling the prevalence of NAFLD and nervous system impairment. For example, the
                    - emphasis [ref=e1489]: Bifidobacterium
                    - text: strains were widely used in different formulas of synbiotics, and the daily intake of synbiotics containing
                    - emphasis [ref=e1490]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1491]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1492]: Lactobacillus casei
                    - text: (7 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1493]: "9"
                    - text: CFU for each) and chicory inulin (100 mg) for 4 months could improve fatty liver grade, inflammatory and antioxidative status in 28 children with obesity-related NAFLD [
                    - link "138" [ref=e1494] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B138-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Moreover, a clinical trial on 79 patients with Alzheimer’s disease revealed that the daily consumption of selenium (200 mg) accompanied with"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1495]: Bifidobacterium bifidum
                    - text: ","
                    - emphasis [ref=e1496]: Bifidobacterium longum
                    - text: ", and"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1497]: Lactobacillus acidophilus
                    - text: (2 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1498]: "9"
                    - text: CFU for each) for 12 weeks could more effectively improve the cognitive function and metabolic function than the single selenium treatment [
                    - link "139" [ref=e1499] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B139-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Furthermore, the combination of"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1500]: Bifidobacterium lactis
                    - text: (5 × 10
                    - superscript [ref=e1501]: "9"
                    - text: CFU/bag) and fructo-oligosaccharides (4.95 g/bag) could improve gastrointestinal discomfort, with decreasing the plasma levels of IL-6, IL-8, IL-17α, and interferon-γ (IFN-γ) [
                    - link "137" [ref=e1502] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B137-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Besides, a meta-analysis showed that the co-treatment of probiotic strains (like"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1503]: Bifidobacterium
                    - text: and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1504]: Lactobacillus
                    - text: ) and prebiotic food (like cheese) revealed the potential to be an effective intervention for metabolic syndrome [
                    - link "149" [ref=e1505] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B149-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1506]:
                    - text: Patients with inflammatory bowel disease were often accompanied with metabolic disorders and the depletion of butyrate-producing bacteria, and the oral intake of butyrate could enhance the efficacy of regular therapy with decreasing the
                    - emphasis [ref=e1507]: Bacteroides fragilis
                    - text: to
                    - emphasis [ref=e1508]: Faecalibacterium prausnitzii
                    - text: ratio [
                    - link "150" [ref=e1509] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B150-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Moreover, hypertension was a common complication of type 2 diabetes mellitus, and the patients who received antihypertensive medications over 3 months showed an increase in butyrate level and a decrease in acetate level, with reducing the levels of circulating fibroblast growth factor 21 (FGF 21), tumor necrosis factor superfamily member 14 (TNFSF 14) and TNF-α ["
                    - link "151" [ref=e1510] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B151-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. Furthermore, sarcopenia is a complication of the elderly diabetic population, and the utilization of butyrate-related derivatives like hydroxyl-methyl butyrate revealed a promising potential in mitigating sarcopenia ["
                    - link "140" [ref=e1511] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B140-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]. In addition, bacillary dysentery is caused by the infection of"
                    - emphasis [ref=e1512]: Shigella
                    - text: germ, which leads to the destruction of the colonic mucosa barrier and metabolic disorders, and the adjunct therapy with butyrate could correct colonic impairment and promote antimicrobial peptides release [
                    - link "141" [ref=e1513] [cursor=pointer]:
                      - /url: "#B141-nutrients-13-03211"
                    - text: "]."
                  - paragraph [ref=e1514]:
                    - text: In summary, PPSP reveal beneficial effects on metabolic-related diseases like nervous system dysfunction, hypertension, and NAFLD, meanwhile, most of the synbiotics formulas are based on
                    - emphasis [ref=e1515]: Bifidobacterium
                    - text: and
                    - emphasis [ref=e1516]: Lactobacillus
                    - text: . Besides, butyrate and related derivates are the most focused postbiotics but still need more clinical assessment.
              - generic [ref=e1517]:
                - heading "5. Conclusions" [level=2] [ref=e1518]: 5. Conclusions
                - paragraph [ref=e1519]:
                  - text: Metabolic diseases are closely associated with gut microbiota dysbiosis. PPSP possess beneficial effects on controlling metabolic diseases such as obesity and type 2 diabetes mellitus by targeting gut microbiota. Since the beneficial effects of PPSP on alleviating metabolic diseases, researchers keep exploiting different compatible solutions for the best therapeutic effect and turns out that the co-intervention of multiple alive bacteria strains could be a promising way. Also, novel food processing strategies like enzyme-modified prebiotics and probiotic-fermented natural foods have been developed to enhance the beneficial effects. Moreover, the in vitro and in vivo studies reveal that the modulation of gut microbiota composition is one of the most direct mechanisms of PPSP to alleviate metabolic disease, manifesting as regulating the abundances of
                  - emphasis [ref=e1520]: Akkermansia
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1521]: Bacteroidetes
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1522]: Blautia
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1523]: Bifidobacteria
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1524]: Bifidobacterium
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1525]: Collinsella
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1526]: Clostridiaceae
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1527]: Christensenellaceae
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1528]: Desulfovibrionaceae
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1529]: Firmicutes
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1530]: Lachnospiraceae
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1531]: Lactobacillus
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1532]: Proteobacteria
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1533]: Rikenellaceae
                  - text: ","
                  - emphasis [ref=e1534]: Ruminococcaceae
                  - text: ", and"
                  - emphasis [ref=e1535]: Yersinia
                  - text: . Furthermore, PPSP also indirectly ameliorate metabolic diseases by regulating gut microbial metabolites, such as acetate, propionate, butyrate, isovalerate, lactic acid, and palmitoylethanolamide, while suppressing the production of LPS and TMAO, as well as reducing the bile acid pools. Additionally, the improvement of the intestinal barrier function plays an important role in attenuating metabolic diseases, with upregulating claudin 1, GLP1, IL-10, occludin 1, and ZO-1 expressions. These mechanisms collectively improve the whole metabolic system by targeting several pivotal signaling pathways, such as upregulating Akt, AMPK, CPR1α, ERK, GPR43, NLCR3, NOD2, GLUT2/4, IRF4, p38 MAPK, PPARα, SIRT1, TLR2, and TRAF6-mediated pathways, while downregulating ACE, NF-κB, NOD1, TLR4, and TNF-α-related pathways. Furthermore, several clinical trials have showed the effects of PPSP on metabolic diseases, and more researches are needed on the situation when patients are during pregnancy. In addition, the optimized formula of synbiotics and the specific efficacy of postbiotics on humans are worthy of further exploration. Besides, the recent researches are mostly focusing on the effects and mechanisms of PPSP on obesity and type 2 diabetes mellitus. In the future, more attention should be paid to other metabolic diseases, such as cardiovascular diseases and hyperuricemia.
